(1) Severity: caret-color is untouched, so the focused input shows the UA caret in --ink-950 on white; WCAG Understanding 2.4.7 explicitly lists the text caret as a valid focus indicator for text fields, so this is a weak-indicator inconsistency (every button gets a 3px ring, the one text field gets only a 1px caret) rather than a hard AA failure; the stated user is touch-first on a phone and unaffected, keyboard being a secondary path (desktop >=1200, tablet with keyboard), and the composer is a fixed prominent pill where the caret is easy to find. P1 'major difficulty' becomes P2. (2) Fix: `rgba(11,101,90,0.35)` blended over the white pill/paper is ~#aac9c5, about 1.8:1 against white, which fails 1.4.11 non-text contrast (3:1) and does not 'match the ring used everywhere else', which is opaque #0b655a (~6.9:1); the finding's 6.74:1 claim applies to the solid color, not the 35% halo. Also `.composer:focus-within` fires when Anexar/Voz/Enviar receive focus, and those buttons already draw their own 3px `:focus-visible` ring, producing a double ring; scoping with `:has(input:focus-visible)` avoids that and `:has()` is already used in this stylesheet (line 388).


### F09 · P2 · ADJUSTED — Dialog consequence text (what an approval does to the memory, case vs rule) is 10-11px

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:2134-2140 (`.dialog-heading p { font-size: 11px; line-height: 1.45 }`), :2160-2166 (`.choice-grid legend { font-size: 10px }`), :2195-2197 (`.choice-grid strong { font-size: 12px }`), :2199-2204 (`.choice-grid small { font-size: 10px; line-height: 1.4 }`), :2222-2228 (`.comparison-grid span { font-size: 9px }`); copy in /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/App.jsx:487-501, 533, 552

**Viewports:** mobile 390 (bottom sheet), desktop 1440 (centered)

**Evidence:** Measured: approve dialog title 20px, option title 12px, helper 10px, legend 10px, description 11px. Strings at 10-11px: 'Defina se a decisão para John Miller vale só agora ou também vira uma regra.', 'Autoriza o pedido de John sem alterar a memória.', 'Cria uma nova orientação operacional aprovada.', 'Como aplicar?'. Colour #48656e on white = 6.25:1 passes; size is the failure. The sheet is `max-height: min(88dvh, 760px)` with `overflow: auto` (:2089-2097), so extra height is absorbed.

**Why:** This is the one place a hurried tap becomes a permanent rule for the business ('Salvar como regra'). The distinction between the two radio options lives entirely in the 10px `small` text; a man on a roof will read the 12px bold titles, skip the 10px explanation, and confirm.

**Fix:** CSS-only, existing tokens, all copy untouched, in /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:
(1) line 2134 `.dialog-heading p { font-size: var(--type-body); line-height: var(--leading-body); }` -> 12.3px @390, 14px @1440 (affects the consequence line of every dialog: approve, adjust, reject, memory-edit, memory-revoke, reset).
(2) line 2195 `.choice-grid strong { font-size: 0.875rem; }` -> 14px (the ceiling of --type-body; keeps a size step above the helper on mobile).
(3) line 2199 `.choice-grid small { font-size: var(--type-small); line-height: var(--leading-body); }` -> 12px/1.5 (keep existing color var(--text-secondary), 6.25:1).
Drop the legend change: `.choice-grid legend` at 10px/800/uppercase is a fieldset kicker consistent with every other uppercase label in the app; at most swap `letter-spacing: 0.08em` for `var(--tracking-label)` as token hygiene. `.comparison-grid span` (9px) belongs to the Memory tab's "Editar regra" dialog and is out of #ligou scope; if touched, `font-size: var(--type-micro)` (10px) as a separate P3. Do not reference `--type-lead` (does not exist). Net height at 390x844: roughly +50-60px inside a sheet whose cap is 743px with ~400px content, so no scrolling is introduced.

**Verifier:** CSS values are exactly as cited (styles.css 2134 `.dialog-heading p` 11px/1.45; 2160 legend 10px/800; 2195 strong 12px; 2199 small 10px/1.4; 2222 `.comparison-grid span` 9px), fixed px with no media-query override, so they hold at 390 and 1440; flow-observations.md item 3 independently recorded 20/12/10; contrast 6.25:1 recomputed (5.7:1 on the selected teal-soft card). Copy quotes match App.jsx:487-501. The legibility problem is real: `.choice-grid small` and `.dialog-heading p` are sentence-case consequence prose rendered at the size the system reserves for uppercase kickers (`--type-micro`), below iOS 11pt / Material 11sp floors, for a phone-in-sunlight user. BUT the stated mechanism is false: App.jsx:443/453 default and reset `approvalMode` to "case", so a hurried "Confirmar aprovação" never creates a rule; the owner must actively tap the self-explanatory 12px bold "Salvar como regra". WCAG 2.2 AA has no minimum font-size SC, so "size is the failure" is a usability/platform-guideline issue, not a WCAG failure. The fix is partly unimplementable and partly a no-op: `--type-lead` does not exist in the dashboard (grep empty, falls back to inherited size); legend `var(--type-micro)` is 10px -> 10px while the finding lists "Como aplicar?" as a failing string (the legend is a fieldset kicker consistent with `.message-label`/`.memory-search-label` at 10px/800 and is not a defect; weight 700 would break the 800 kicker convention); `.comparison-grid span` is in the Memory-tab "Editar regra" dialog, outside the #ligou scope. Height estimate (~30px) is low (~50-60px at 390 because both `small` lines wrap at 12.3px in a ~274px column) but irrelevant: sheet cap is 743px vs ~400px content, so no scroll occurs.


### F10 · P2 · ADJUSTED — Decision sheet hides the facts being decided (65% navy backdrop + blur over the card) and the rule scope is chosen without seeing which facts become the rule

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:2077-2087 (.dialog-backdrop rgba(4,29,39,.65) + backdrop-filter blur(5px)); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/App.jsx:487 (approve dialog description names only the client), :503-520 (.rule-options Escopo/Duração)

**Viewports:** mobile 390

**Evidence:** Approve sheet ~349-360px tall anchored at y 832 covers y ~472-832; the card sits at y 517-769 at scroll 0 or ~367-619 after the required scroll, so 'Antes das 17h · Problema na chaminé', 'Hoje · 15/08/2026' and the rule are behind the sheet or blurred. The dialog shows 'Defina se a decisão para John Miller vale só agora…' and, when 'Salvar como regra' is chosen, asks Escopo and Duração without showing which facts become the rule.

**Why:** Recognition over recall: the owner must hold four facts in working memory while choosing between case and rule. Rafael in the sun will confirm from memory of a card he read at 11-12px.

**Fix:** Approve dialog only (App.jsx ~:487-520, `dialog.type === "approve"`). Inside the existing `<form className="dialog-form">`, before `<fieldset className="choice-grid">`, echo the facts using the dialog system's existing paper-warm pattern instead of a new class: `<div className="comparison-grid"><div><span>Solicitação</span><p>{approval.request}{approval.note ? ` · ${approval.note}` : ""}</p></div><div><span>Regra consultada</span><p>{approval.rule}</p></div><div><span>Nova proposta</span><p>{approval.proposedAction}</p></div></div>`. All three labels are strings already in the product ('Solicitação' = ApprovalCard sr-only dt, 'Regra consultada' = .approval-context span / CausalStrip small, 'Nova proposta' = adjust-dialog label); values are existing data fields (fixtures.js:152-155). `.comparison-grid > div` (styles.css:2211-2218) already gives var(--paper-warm) surface, 1px var(--border), 12px radius, 9px uppercase label + 12px/1.5 body — no new CSS tokens, light theme, Archivo body, no orange, teal untouched. Do not repeat clientName (already in the description). Sheet growth: rule mode ~513px + ~150px echo ≈ 660px < max-height min(88dvh,760px)=743px at 844, panel has overflow:auto and a sticky heading, so nothing breaks; at >=768 .comparison-grid becomes 2 columns and the third card wraps, acceptable. DROP the backdrop change (rgba .45 / no blur): measured to be irrelevant to the hidden facts in rule mode and it would change the scrim of every mobile dialog. Leave .dialog-backdrop as is. Optional refinement if RJ wants the sheet shorter: render the echo only when `approvalMode === "rule"`, directly above `.rule-options`, since that is the moment the facts become the rule; but the always-on variant is preferred because at scroll 0 the case-mode sheet (y 483) also covers the whole card (y 517-769).

**Verifier:** Evidence is real and I re-measured it live at 390x844 on the prototype (own tab, real Aprovar tap): .dialog-backdrop computes to rgba(4,29,39,0.65) + blur(5px) (styles.css:2077-2087, no mobile override; the >=768 block at :2962 only changes align-items/padding); case-mode sheet = y 483-832, h 349 (matches the claimed ~349); rule-mode sheet grows to y 319-832, h 513, and every .approval-facts row measures 'behind-sheet' in rule mode at the scrolled state (card rows y 380-538). The 'why' is stronger than stated: (a) .mobile-approval .approval-context {display:none} (styles.css:1035) hides 'Regra consultada' on the mobile card, so the rule exists only on the causal strip at 8.31px (y ~221-277 after scroll), under the scrim+blur; (b) the text that literally becomes the saved rule is approval.proposedAction (model.js:411-413 requireText(options.ruleText || options.text || approval.proposedAction); gateway.supabase.js:270 same fallback) and App.jsx passes only {mode, scope, duration}, so the owner saves a permanent rule whose text ('Abrir um encaixe para atendimento ainda hoje.') is never shown in the dialog nor on the mobile card; scope options 'Este serviço'/'Esta localização' refer to facts (chaminé, San Rafael) not visible either. What is off: (1) the backdrop half of the fix does nothing for the decision facts — in rule mode the whole card is behind the sheet regardless of scrim opacity, and in case mode only kicker/client/location/date peek above (client is already in the description); it would also alter the scrim of every mobile dialog (Voz, Memória, reset), which is not surgical to this finding. (2) The fix targets approve/adjust/reject, but adjust already pre-fills proposedAction in the textarea and reject already names the client; only the approve dialog has the gap. (3) 'No new strings' is not true of a bare <dl> — dt labels are needed; they must reuse existing strings. (4) The echo should include proposedAction, the field that actually becomes the rule, which the original fix omits. Severity P2 stands: default path 'Somente este caso' needs only the client name (present), the sheet is cancellable, so not P1; but a permanent memory write made blind justifies more than P3. Prior QA (design-qa.md:51) only recorded a functional pass, no IA judgment, so nothing already accepted conflicts.


### F11 · P3 · ADJUSTED — Approve/Adjust/Reject/Restaurar dialogs open with focus on the 'Fechar' X — the exit is announced and ringed before the question

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/components/Dialog.jsx:22-25 ((focusables[0] || panel).focus()) and :79-88 (icon-button is the first focusable in DOM order); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:215-218 (:focus-visible 3px ring, offset 3px), :301-312 (.icon-button 44x44, no border-radius)

**Viewports:** all (measured 390 and 1440)

**Evidence:** Measured on Aprovar, Recusar and Restaurar dialogs at 390 and 1440: document.activeElement = button.icon-button[aria-label='Fechar'] every time (rect 316-360 x 503-547 on the mobile sheet). Focusable order in the panel: Fechar -> radio 'case' -> radio 'rule' -> Cancelar -> Confirmar. With keyboard-initiated opens Chrome shows the 3px ring (--focus-ring-color #0b655a, outline-offset 3px) around a square X — the 'glitch' recorded in flow-observations.md item 3 (seen in Ajustar and Restaurar dialogs). The X is also the farthest control from the thumb on a sheet whose actions sit at y 770. All three lenses rated P2.

**Why:** Screen readers announce 'Fechar, botão' before the title 'Aprovar esta decisão'; Enter at that moment closes the sheet. Sighted keyboard users see a teal box around an X as the sheet rises, reading as an error at the exact moment of decision.

**Fix:** Dialog.jsx:25 -> `panel?.focus()` (panel already has tabIndex -1, aria-labelledby and aria-describedby, so title + description are announced on entry). Dialog.jsx:42 -> `if (event.shiftKey && (document.activeElement === first || document.activeElement === panel))` so Shift+Tab from the freshly focused panel wraps to `last` instead of escaping to the app behind the backdrop. styles.css: add `.dialog-panel:focus-visible { outline: none; }` (container is not in the tab sequence; APG-sanctioned). Do NOT reorder the X after .dialog-content: .dialog-heading is `position: sticky` inside the scrolling .dialog-panel, so an absolutely positioned X on the panel would detach from the heading when the dialog scrolls; keep the current DOM, where the first Tab after open lands on the 44px X (APG 'static element first, close is first tabbable'). Do NOT use the 'first focusable inside .dialog-content' alternative: in Ajustar and Editar regra it focuses a <textarea> and opens the Android keyboard over the sheet on open. Optional polish: `.icon-button { border-radius: var(--radius-sm); }` (8px, on the token scale; the Dialog X is the only .icon-button consumer, so no side effects). No copy changes; aria-label 'Fechar' stays.

**Verifier:** Mechanics are real: Dialog.jsx:25 focuses focusables[0], and the 'Fechar' X (line 84) precedes .dialog-content in DOM; :focus-visible is a 3px #0b655a ring (styles.css:215-218); .icon-button is 44x44 unrounded (301-312); the 316-360 x 503-547 rect matches backdrop padding 12 + heading padding 18. But the impact is inflated. (a) The stated user (owner, thumb, phone outdoors) never sees it: on a touch/mouse-initiated open, Chrome/Safari do not match :focus-visible on script-moved focus, so no ring appears; the 'glitch' in flow-observations item 3 was produced by JS-driven clicks in the internal browser, where script focus with no prior interaction does match. (b) For keyboard users a ring on the focused control is REQUIRED by WCAG 2.4.7/2.4.11 and initial focus on the first focusable is the APG default; no 2.4.3 failure exists. (c) With role=dialog aria-modal aria-labelledby aria-describedby, NVDA/JAWS/VoiceOver-macOS announce title+description on entry before 'Fechar, botão'; only iOS VO is inconsistent. (d) design-qa.md:60 already accepted 'Diálogos com foco inicial, Escape e retorno de foco: passou'. So this is a polish preference (P3), not an annoyance for the target user. The proposed fix also has two defects: the trap at Dialog.jsx:42 only intercepts Shift+Tab when activeElement === first, so after panel.focus() Shift+Tab leaks to the AppShell behind the backdrop (the claim 'the existing trap' covers it is false); and moving the X after .dialog-content with position:absolute on .dialog-panel breaks stickiness, because .dialog-heading is sticky INSIDE the scrolling .dialog-panel (overflow:auto; max-height:min(88dvh,760px)) and an absolute child of the scroll container scrolls away in tall dialogs (Editar regra at 320x700, cap 616px). The 'alternative' would focus a textarea in Ajustar/Editar and pop the Android keyboard over the sheet on open. .icon-button has one consumer (grep), so a radius is safe, but 10px is off the 8/12/20/28 token scale.


### F12 · P3 · ADJUSTED — No pressed state anywhere: tap highlight removed, no :active rule in the file, hover is a 1px lift only, composer/dialog controls have no state feedback

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:164-170 (-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent on button/a/input), :3280-3301 (@media (hover:hover) — transition + translateY(-1px) for .button/.approval-actions button only), .composer-send :1228, .voice-demo-button :1233, .composer-attachment :1223, .icon-button/.profile-button :302-327, .toast button :2283, .profile-menu-panel button :359; grep ':active' = 0 matches

**Viewports:** mobile 390, tablet 768, desktop 1440

**Evidence:** Measured webkitTapHighlightColor rgba(0,0,0,0) on .approval-actions button; `(hover: hover)` is false at 390 so tapping Aprovar produces zero visual change until the sheet finishes rising 240ms later (instantly, with no transition, under reduced motion). At 1440 hovering Aprovar/Ajustar/Recusar changes only transform (computed transition 'color .14s, background-color .14s, border-color .14s, transform .14s' but no rule alters colour/border for .button--primary or .button--outline-dark). .composer-send, .voice-demo-button, .icon-button, .toast button, .profile-button: transition 'all 0s', no :hover/:active/:focus styles beyond the global ring.

**Why:** Outdoors, with gloves or a wet thumb, the owner cannot tell whether 'Aprovar' registered; he taps again (the busy guard saves the data, not the confidence). On desktop the orange CTA feels inert.

**Fix:** All CSS-only, no copy touched. In /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:

A) Inside the existing `@media (hover: hover) { ... }` block (3280-3301): extend the transition list at 3281-3288 with `.composer-send, .voice-demo-button, .composer-attachment, .icon-button, .profile-button, .toast button, .profile-menu-panel button` and change it to `transition: color var(--dur-fast) var(--ease-out), background-color var(--dur-fast) var(--ease-out), border-color var(--dur-fast) var(--ease-out), transform var(--dur-fast) var(--ease-out);`. Add hover tints there too (keeps the approved orange family, no --orange-dark surface): `.button--primary:hover:not(:disabled) { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--orange), #000 10%); }` `.button--outline-dark:hover:not(:disabled) { border-color: rgba(255,255,255,0.9); background: rgba(255,255,255,0.06); }` `.voice-demo-button:hover { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--teal-soft), var(--teal) 12%); }` `.icon-button:hover, .toast button:hover, .profile-menu-panel button:hover { background: rgba(4,29,39,0.06); }` `.profile-button:hover { background: rgba(255,255,255,0.10); }`.

B) AFTER the closing brace of that hover block (after line 3301) and BEFORE `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` at 3303, add the press states outside any media query so they fire on touch and win the source-order tie against the hover lift: `.button:active:not(:disabled), .approval-actions button:active:not(:disabled), .composer-send:active, .voice-demo-button:active, .composer-attachment:active, .icon-button:active, .profile-button:active, .toast button:active, .profile-menu-panel button:active { transform: translateY(0) scale(0.97); }` `.button--primary:active:not(:disabled) { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--orange), #000 14%); }` `.button--outline-dark:active:not(:disabled) { background: rgba(255,255,255,0.10); }` `.composer-send:active { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--orange), #000 14%); }` `.destination-link:active .destination-icon { transform: scale(0.92); }`. Leave `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` at 169 in place (now replaced by a designed press state). The reduced-motion block already collapses transition-duration to 0.01ms, so the scale/tint press feedback still shows instantly there.

**Verifier:** The raw CSS facts hold: `grep ':active'` on styles.css returns 0 matches; lines 164-170 set `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on button/a/input/textarea/select; the only interactive feedback is the `@media (hover: hover)` block at 3280-3301 (translateY(-1px) on .button/.approval-actions button/.memory-card-actions button, border-color teal on list items); .composer-send (1228), .voice-demo-button (1233), .composer-attachment (1223), .icon-button/.profile-button (301-317), .toast button (2283) and .profile-menu-panel button (359) declare no hover/active/transition. But the evidence and the harm are overstated. (1) "Zero visual change until the sheet finishes rising 240ms later" is false: ApprovalCard's Aprovar calls `openApproval` -> `setDialog({type:'approve'})` (App.jsx:286), React flushes the discrete event synchronously, and `.dialog-backdrop` (styles.css:2077-2087, fixed inset:0, rgba(4,29,39,0.65) + blur, NO animation) paints on the very next frame; only `.dialog-panel` animates, and it starts at opacity 0.4 not 0. The whole screen dims within one frame of the tap, and the backdrop blocks any second tap on Aprovar, so the "taps again" scenario cannot occur on the card. (2) "The busy guard saves the data, not the confidence" is misattributed: the busy guard lives on the sheet's submit buttons (App.jsx:450-467, 523/543/557 `disabled={busy}`), and `button:disabled { opacity: 0.52 }` (styles.css:229-232) dims that button on the same frame the tap is handled, which IS state feedback. (3) Every other control named has immediate consequence feedback: composer-send appends the bubble and swaps back to the voice pill (flow-observations #6 "smooth"), voice pill opens a backdrop dialog, icon-button closes the dialog, toast button dismisses the toast, profile-button shows the panel, destination-link moves the is-active orange underline. What is genuinely missing is only the press-down (pre-release) affordance, made more noticeable because the OS tap highlight was removed without a replacement; the desktop hover on the orange CTA is a lift only (the file itself is inconsistent: .memory-approve-button:hover at 1693 does darken). WCAG 2.2 AA has no SC that requires a pressed state, so that source is decorative. Net: real polish gap, not a P2 annoyance that costs the owner confidence -> P3. The fix also needs three corrections: source order (an :active rule placed before the hover block ties on specificity (0,3,0) with `.button:hover:not(:disabled)` and loses on desktop while the pointer is down), hover rules must go inside the existing `(hover: hover)` block to avoid sticky hover on touch, and `.button--primary:active { background: var(--orange-dark) }` paints the approve action in the same #B83216 used by `.button--danger` (1106-1109) and reserved by the brand guide p.17 for small text on paper.


### F13 · P2 · ADJUSTED — Bottom sheet jumps 164px upward the instant 'Salvar como regra' is selected

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/App.jsx:503-520 (.rule-options mounted conditionally); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:2089-2108 (dialog-rise only on mount; no height/row transition), :2160-2178 (.choice-grid label.is-selected, no transition)

**Viewports:** mobile 390

**Evidence:** Panel rect before: y 483.1, h 348.9; after clicking the second radio: y 319.1, h 512.9 (Δ 164px) — .rule-options (h 146) appears with animationName none, transition none; the heading 'Aprovar esta decisão' relocates under the thumb in one frame. The label's teal fill also flips with no transition.

**Why:** On a phone the sheet is anchored to the bottom, so growth moves the top edge and everything the eye was on. Motion here should explain 'more options appeared', not teleport the sheet.

**Fix:** App.jsx:503-520 - render unconditionally: <div className="rule-options-reveal" data-open={approvalMode === "rule"} inert={approvalMode !== "rule"}><div className="rule-options">...the same two labels/selects with the same strings...</div></div> (React 19.2.0 accepts boolean inert; aria-hidden is redundant with inert). styles.css, next to .rule-options at :2206: .rule-options-reveal { display: grid; grid-template-rows: 0fr; overflow: hidden; margin-top: -18px; padding-inline: 4px; margin-inline: -4px; transition: grid-template-rows var(--dur-med) var(--ease-out); } .rule-options-reveal[data-open="true"] { grid-template-rows: 1fr; } .rule-options-reveal > .rule-options { min-height: 0; padding-top: 18px; } - the margin-top: -18px plus inner padding-top: 18px folds the .dialog-form 18px gap into the collapsing element so the default state keeps exactly today's 18px between the choice cards and the buttons; the 4px inline padding/negative margin stops overflow:hidden from clipping the selects' focus ring. At :2168 add to .choice-grid label: transition: border-color var(--dur-fast) var(--ease-out), background-color var(--dur-fast) var(--ease-out);. Leave the >=768 rule .rule-options { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0,1fr)) } at :2971 untouched (it applies to the inner element). No reduced-motion addition needed: @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) at :3303 already forces transition-duration 0.01ms on *.

**Verifier:** Evidence is real and reproduced in the running prototype (http://localhost:4180/dashboard/#ligou at 390x844): .dialog-panel h 348.9 -> 512.9 (delta 164.0), panel bottom fixed (backdrop align-items: flex-end confirmed at styles.css:2077-2087), .rule-options h 146 with animationName none and transition duration 0s; .dialog-form gap 18px makes 146+18 = 164 exactly. Root cause confirmed in source: App.jsx:503-520 mounts .rule-options conditionally, .dialog-panel (styles.css:2089-2097) only animates on mount, .choice-grid label (:2168-2181) has no transition so the teal fill flips instantly. The critic's y values are 14px off mine (497.1 vs 483.1) but heights and delta match, so the measurement stands. Two adjustments: (1) evidence wording - the heading moves 164px UP (y 517 -> 353); what lands where the finger tapped 'Salvar como regra' (label y 702-762) is the Duração <select> (elementFromPoint returned SELECT 'Permanente/Temporária'), while .dialog-actions stays at y 780 - so the primary button does not move, but the tapped card flies away and a form control replaces it. (2) The proposed fix has an implementation defect: an always-rendered wrapper becomes a permanent .dialog-form grid child, so in the default 'Somente este caso' state the collapsed 0px wrapper still contributes gap -> 36px between the choice cards and the buttons instead of 18px (sheet 18px taller by default, regression). Fix corrected below. Severity P2 stands: an instant reflow of 19% of the viewport on the product's core loop (aprovação -> regra), annoyance not blocker; reduced-motion is already handled by the global * rule at :3303-3315 (transition-duration 0.01ms !important), so that claim is true.


### F14 · P3 · ADJUSTED — Confirmation toast lands at the top, covering the element that just changed (inspector heading on desktop, heading/avatar on mobile) and sitting over the profile panel, with no motion

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:2255-2275 (.toast fixed top: calc(var(--header-height) + 10px), z 150), :2969-2974 (desktop top 18px right 18px width 420px), :333-337 (.profile-menu-panel z-index 70); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/App.jsx:35-53 (4200ms, no motion)

**Viewports:** mobile 390, desktop 1440

**Evidence:** Desktop 1440x900 after approving: .toast rect x 1002-1422, y 18-84; .inspector-kicker 'AGORA' y 38-48 and inspector h2 'Sua decisão é necessária' y 59-89 fully hidden for 4.2s. Mobile: toast y 72-138 covers the agent avatar/bubble at scrollY 152-198 and the h1 at scrollY 0; .profile-menu-panel rect y 60.5-177 overlaps the toast band and loses (z 70 < 150). Thumb and eyes are at y ~700 when the toast lands at y 72 with its 44px close button top-right. Computed animationName none, transition none: it pops in and out. The chat column at 1440 has ~286px of measured empty paper (y 528-814) that could host it. Severity: interaction lens P2 (0.93) vs IA lens P3; P2 kept on the measured occlusion of the inspector state title.

**Why:** The owner looks at the panel heading and the card to confirm what changed; the banner hides exactly that for four seconds. Feedback far from the action is missed by a distracted user, and the result ('Decisão aprovada somente para este caso.') is the moment of relief the product is selling.

**Fix:** Keep the copy and the toast component; change only position/motion/duration. (1) Desktop with inspector — inside `@media (min-width: 1200px)`: `.app-shell.has-inspector .toast { top: auto; bottom: 24px; right: 24px; left: auto; width: 376px; }` — this drops the toast into the ~458px of empty navy under the inspector card (card ends y 442 at 1440x900; toast lands y ~810-876), adjacent to the card that just changed, clear of the kicker/h2 and clear of the chat column's newest message. (2) Mobile: keep the top anchor (it hides only the static h1/avatar band and never the newest message or the card); do not move it above the composer. (3) Motion: add `@keyframes toast-in { from { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(-8px); } }` and `.toast { animation: toast-in var(--dur-med) var(--ease-out) both; }` (use `translateY(8px)` in the ≥1200 has-inspector rule since it rises from the bottom there); the existing `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block at styles.css:3303 already zeroes animation-duration. (4) App.jsx:42: 4200 -> 3200ms for `kind: "success"` only (the inline `.system-message` persists with the same news); keep 4200ms for warnings, which have no inline twin.

**Verifier:** Evidence checks out against the source: `.toast` (styles.css:2255) is fixed at top calc(62px+10px)=72px on mobile, and the `@media (min-width:768px)` override (styles.css:2976-2981, not 2969-2974) puts it at top 18 / right 18 / width min(420px, calc(100vw-110px)) = 420px, i.e. x 1002-1422 at 1440 — squarely over the inspector (x 1016-1440), whose `.inspector-content` has padding 32px, kicker 9px, h2 clamp(22px,2vw,30px)=28.8px at line-height 1.05 (y 59-89) before the card at y 138. No `animation`/`transition` is declared on `.toast` (the dialog gets `dialog-rise`, the toast does not), and App.jsx:42 hard-codes 4200ms. The z-order overlap with `.profile-menu-panel` (z 70 inside the z-50 sticky header) is real but only when both are open at once — an edge case, and the toast winning is the correct order. The "why" is overstated: the toast never covers the card (card top 138 > toast bottom 84), and the inspector h2 only changes when the LAST pending item is decided (App.jsx:309 flips to "Operação dentro das regras"); in the fixture flow (John -> Maria) the covered title is unchanged text. The confirmation is also delivered inline by the `.system-message` (ChatView.jsx:27) and by the toast text itself, so nothing is lost. By the brief's scale this is polish (P3), not an annoyance (P2). The proposed fix has two defects: `width: min(420px, 640px)` is a no-op typo, and bottom-anchoring above the composer collides with the newest message — the mobile auto-scroll anchor (`.conversation-end` scroll-margin-bottom = 77+116 = 193px) parks the freshly added "Você aprovou…" system message with its bottom at <= y 651 while the proposed toast (bottom nav+70 = 147px, ~66px tall) occupies y 631-697, hiding the very inline confirmation it relies on; on desktop the same happens once the conversation grows past the 900px dead zone.


### F15 · P2 · CONFIRMED — Composer placeholder 'Fale com o Ligou...' is #798b92 on white = 3.55:1, below AA and the only sub-4.5 running text on paper

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:1205-1207 (.composer input::placeholder { color: #798b92 }); input at :1193-1203 is 16px on .composer background rgba(255,255,255,.97) (:1188)

**Viewports:** all

**Evidence:** Computed WCAG #798b92 / #ffffff = 3.55:1 at 16px/400 (AA needs 4.5:1). Every other secondary grey on paper passes: #48656e 6.06-6.25:1, #5b727c 4.92:1, #31515c on #eaf7f2 7.75:1, #344e5a 8.81:1. 'Fale com o Ligou...' is the only text in the 366x56 fixed bar until the owner types. (Chrome does not expose ::placeholder via getComputedStyle, so this is the stylesheet value, not a live read.) All three lenses rated P2.

**Why:** The placeholder is the instruction that tells him he can just type to the agent; in daylight on a glossy screen a 3.5:1 grey disappears and the pill reads as an empty white bar with a mic button.

**Fix:** `.composer input::placeholder { color: var(--text-secondary); /* #48656e, 6.25:1 on white */ opacity: 1; }` — or `var(--text-muted)` (#5b727c, 4.92:1, annotated 'fica AA' at :72) as the floor. Copy unchanged.

**Verifier:** Evidence checks out against the source. `/Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:1205-1207` is exactly `.composer input::placeholder { color: #798b92; }`; it is the only `::placeholder` rule in the 3316-line file and no media query (387, 2557, 2678, 2692, 2984, 3003, 3223) overrides it, so the stylesheet value is what renders at every viewport. The input is 16px (styles.css:1202) on `.composer` background rgba(255,255,255,.97) over --paper #fbfcf8, i.e. effectively white. Recomputed WCAG 2.x luminance contrast: #798b92 on #ffffff = 3.55:1, on #fbfcf8 = 3.44:1 — below the 4.5:1 AA floor for normal-size text (16px/400 is not "large text"). The comparison greys also recompute as claimed: #48656e 6.25/6.06, #5b727c 5.07/4.92, #31515c 8.53, #344e5a 8.81, #51636b 6.27; the only other lower-contrast hex on the page (#c2cdd1 at :297, .business-compact span) is on the navy sidebar, not on paper, so "only sub-4.5 running text on paper" holds. The placeholder string is set in `views/ChatView.jsx:236` (`placeholder="Fale com o Ligou..."`) and is the only visible text in the 366x56 fixed composer (mobile geometry JSON) until the owner types; the sr-only label at :230 helps screen readers but not sunlight. The file's own token comment (styles.css:66-67) states the deliberate policy that muted text on paper stays AA — this hard-coded hex silently breaks that policy. Severity P2 is fair: the pill shape plus mic button still signal "type here", so it is an annoyance/AA compliance miss, not a task blocker; not inflated, not P3 either because it is a real WCAG 1.4.3 failure on the primary input of the view.


### F16 · P3 · ADJUSTED — Decision card headline (kicker) is 9px and, in the desktop inspector, the rule that justifies the exception is 10px regular under an 8px label

**Viewports:** desktop 1440, mobile 390 (kicker), mobile 320 (kicker)

**Evidence:** Measured 1440x900 inside .context-inspector: `.approval-kicker span` 9px / 800 / 1.26px tracking, #ff7959 on #082f3d = 5.48:1; `.approval-context span` 'Regra consultada' 8px / 800 / 0.96px tracking, #9fb6be on #082f3d = 6.68:1; `.approval-context p` 'Encaixe no mesmo dia exige sua aprovação.' 10px / 400, #eaf0f2, 312px wide. Same card: buttons 13px, facts 13px. Mobile 360: kicker 9px, 179px wide. Contrast passes; size does not.

**Why:** 'PEDIDO URGENTE · MESMO DIA' is the card's headline — it tells the owner what kind of exception this is — and it is set smaller than the timestamps; orange 800 caps at 9px with wide tracking read as a dotted line. On desktop the inspector is where he decides; the rule text there is 10px regular light-grey on navy, thin strokes that haze in bright light.

**Fix:** In /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css, align the card to its existing siblings instead of inventing new values: (a) `.approval-kicker { font-size: var(--type-micro); letter-spacing: 0.13em; }` (9 -> 10px, identical to `.approvals-detail-kicker`; keep font-weight 800 and #ff7959, 5.48:1 passes); (b) `.approval-context span { font-size: var(--type-micro); letter-spacing: var(--tracking-label); }` (8 -> 10px, keep 800 and #9fb6be); (c) `.approval-context p { font-size: var(--type-body); line-height: var(--leading-compact); }` (10 -> 14px at >=1200 where it is visible, matching `.approvals-decision-block p` and the facts' 1.35 leading). No JSX or copy changes. Card grows ~11px in the inspector, which has ~458px of unused navy below it; mobile card grows 1px (kicker only, since .approval-context is display:none there).

**Verifier:** Evidence checks out against styles.css: `.approval-kicker` (l.960-970) is 9px/800/0.14em = 1.26px tracking in #ff7959; `.approval-context span` (l.1039-1045) is 8px/800/0.12em = 0.96px; `.approval-context p` (l.1047-1052) is 10px/400 #eaf0f2, and `.mobile-approval .approval-context {display:none}` (l.1035) makes the span/p desktop-inspector-only. Recomputed contrast: 5.48:1 and 6.68:1, exactly as stated. But three things are off. (1) The 'why' overstates the kicker: in the approved mock (853px wide, ~2.19x of 390) 'PEDIDO URGENTE · MESMO DIA' is drawn as a small eyebrow at roughly 8-8.5px equivalent, so 9px is faithful to the approved hierarchy, not a shrunken headline; the card's real headline is the facts block (12.3px at 390, 14px at >=1200 — the finding's '13px' is --type-chat, not --type-body, which clamps to 14px at 1440). The genuine defect on the kicker is internal inconsistency: the same eyebrow on the Approvals page, `.approvals-detail-kicker` (l.1917-1923), already uses var(--type-micro) with the same #ff7959/800. (2) The 10px rule text is real but desktop-only (>=1200), so the 'bright light on a phone' rationale does not apply to it, and the same sentence is already on screen at 13px in the agent bubble and in the causal strip — the inspector line is a third, redundant instance. Sibling `.approvals-decision-block p` (l.1996-2000) uses var(--type-body), so raising it is codebase-consistent. (3) The fix has wrong numbers: it labels var(--type-micro) as '/* 11px */' but the token is 0.625rem = 10px (l.89), and '13 -> 15px' for var(--type-body) is impossible — the clamp maxes at 14px (l.91); at 1440 it is 14px. Weight 700 is loaded (l.25-30) but would drift from every sibling kicker (all 800). Net: a real but polish-level legibility/consistency issue on a redundant desktop element plus a 1px token alignment on the kicker — P3, not P2.


### F17 · P2 · ADJUSTED — Chrome text around the conversation is 8-11px: header owner line 10px, sidebar company label 8px, reset 10px, inspector helper 11px, inspector kicker 9px, profile menu 9-10px, sending status 9px

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:295-299 (`.business-compact span { font-size: 10px; color: #c2cdd1 }`; :2596-2598 -> 9px at ≤359), :3037-3043 (`.business-card span { font-size: 8px; letter-spacing: 0.12em }`), :3050-3053 (`.business-card small { font-size: 10px }`), :3075-3080 (`.reset-button { font-size: 10px }`), :3112-3118 (`.inspector-kicker` 9px), :3127-3132 (`.inspector-content > p { font-size: 11px; color: #adc0c7 }`), :353-357 (`.profile-menu-panel span { font-size: 9px }`), :359-373 (`.profile-menu-panel button { font-size: 10px }`), :1246-1259 (`.sending-status { font-size: 9px }`)

**Viewports:** mobile 390 (header, profile menu, sending status), desktop 1440 (sidebar, inspector)

**Evidence:** Measured 360: `.business-compact span` 'Rafael · Proprietário' 10px, #c2cdd1 on #082f3d = 8.72:1. Measured 1440: `.business-card span` 'Empresa' 8px / 800 / 0.96px tracking, #90a8b0 on #082f3d = 5.67:1; `.business-card small` 10px; `.inspector-kicker` 'AGORA' 9px #46c2af 7.93:1; `.reset-button span` 'Restaurar demonstração' 10px; `.inspector-content > p` 'O Ligou reuniu o contexto e não improvisou diante da exceção.' 11px / 17px line-height, #adc0c7 on #041d27 = 9.21:1. Contrast passes everywhere; size is the issue. On the 1440 canvas the sidebar is 236px and the inspector 424px wide — no space pressure justifies 8-11px. Severity: typography lens P2, a11y lens P3; P2 kept because the inspector paragraph is the sentence that delivers the brand's emotion target.

**Why:** 'não improvisou' = relief with control, set at 11px light grey on navy — the eye goes to the 28.8px h2 and never reaches it. 'Rafael · Proprietário' at 10px is acceptable as tertiary, but 'Dados salvos somente neste navegador.' at 9px in the profile menu is a legal-ish disclosure nobody will read.

**Fix:** All in /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css, copy untouched, colors untouched. Base rules: `.business-compact span { font-size: var(--type-small); }` (10 -> 12px; keep the <=359 override at :2596 but set it to `var(--type-micro)` = 10px instead of 9px). `.profile-menu-panel span { font-size: var(--type-body); }` (9 -> 12.3px at 390; keep line-height 1.4); `.profile-menu-panel button { font-size: var(--type-body); }` (10 -> 12.3px); leave `.profile-menu-panel strong` at 12px or set `var(--type-body)`. `.sending-status { font-size: var(--type-small); }` (9 -> 12px). Inside the existing `@media (min-width: 1200px)` block: `.business-card span { font-size: var(--type-micro); font-weight: 700; letter-spacing: var(--tracking-label); }` (8 -> 10px uppercase label; 10px is acceptable for a 7-letter structural kicker); `.business-card small { font-size: var(--type-small); }` (10 -> 12px); `.reset-button { font-size: var(--type-body); }` (10 -> 14px, label only visible >=1360); `.inspector-kicker { font-size: var(--type-micro); }` (9 -> 10px); `.inspector-content > p { font-size: var(--type-body); line-height: var(--leading-body); max-width: 40ch; }` (11/17px -> 14/21px). Do not use --type-micro for the header owner line or sidebar small — it is already their current 10px value.

**Verifier:** Evidence is real: every cited size matches styles.css in the worktree (.business-compact span 10px :297 / 9px at <=359 :2597; .business-card span 8px 800 0.12em :3037-3043; .business-card small 10px :3052; .reset-button 10px at >=1200 :3079; .inspector-kicker 9px :3114; .inspector-content > p 11px x 1.55 = 17.05px :3130-3131; .profile-menu-panel span 9px :355, button 10px :370; .sending-status 9px :1258), and all four contrast ratios recompute exactly (8.72, 5.67, 7.93 on #041d27, 9.21). Two corrections. (1) The FIX is broken by a token error: --type-micro is 0.625rem = 10px (:89), not 11px as stated, so '.business-compact span -> var(--type-micro)' and '.business-card small -> var(--type-micro)' are no-ops (already 10px), and kicker/sending-status would only move 9->10px. The correct next step on the existing scale is --type-small (12px, :90), which the dashboard already uses for tertiary labels (date, nav links, .profile-menu-panel strong). (2) The P2 rationale is anchored on the desktop inspector paragraph, which only renders at >=1200 for a phone-first user; the stronger case is .profile-menu-panel span at 9px on mobile, which in real (non-prototype) mode reads 'Zera conversa, onboarding, memória, aprovações e poderes de teste.' — the explanation of a destructive reset, read on a phone in daylight. P2 stands on that basis; the header owner line and sidebar 'Empresa' label alone would be P3. Fit verified after correction: header is 62px min-height minus 8px padding = 46px; 13px strong + 2px + 12px span ~33px. Profile panel is min(270px, 100vw-24px); --type-body at 390 = 12.31px so icon + 'Restaurar demonstração' fits. .reset-button span is display:none at 768-1359 (:2787, :3244-3246), so the >=1200 font-size only matters at >=1360 and cannot break the rail. Inspector p at 14px/21px with max-width 40ch (~320px) fits the 376px inner width.


### F18 · P3 · ADJUSTED — 'Respondendo…' status is 9px and, in the prototype, exists for 9ms — the only 'I heard you' signal is never painted

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:1246-1259 (.sending-status font-size 9px, absolute right 16px above the composer); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/views/ChatView.jsx:263; /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/App.jsx:272-284

**Viewports:** all (measured 390)

**Evidence:** MutationObserver during a real send at 390: .sending-status mounted 11.4ms after submit and unmounted 8.9ms later (rect 264-361 x 674-696, font-size 9px, no animation); the send button's :disabled state lasted the same window. Nothing is ever painted; in real (Supabase) mode the same 9px label will be the only in-flight feedback.

**Why:** This is the only signal between Enter and the reply; at 9px it fails the outdoor-phone brief and it appears/disappears with no motion so it reads as flicker.

**Fix:** styles.css, replace the `.sending-status` block (1246-1259) and add one keyframe next to `dialog-rise` — no JSX/copy change, "Respondendo…" and the icon stay:

```css
.sending-status {
  position: absolute;
  right: 16px;
  bottom: calc(100% + 6px);
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 6px;
  min-height: 28px;
  padding: 6px 10px;
  border-radius: 8px;
  color: var(--text-secondary);
  background: var(--white);
  box-shadow: var(--shadow-soft);
  font-size: var(--type-small); /* 12px; switch to var(--type-micro) only if F05 raises it to >=12px */
  font-weight: 600;
  /* Appears only if the send is still pending after 120ms: instant replies
     (prototype) never flash it; network sends get a real entrance. */
  animation: status-in var(--dur-med) var(--ease-out) 120ms both;
}

.sending-status svg {
  flex: 0 0 auto;
  animation: status-breathe 1.6s ease-in-out infinite;
}

@keyframes status-in {
  from { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(6px); }
  to   { opacity: 1; transform: none; }
}

@keyframes status-breathe {
  0%, 100% { opacity: 1; }
  50%      { opacity: 0.45; }
}
```

Do NOT add the `Promise.all([..., setTimeout 350])` hold in App.jsx sendMessage — the prototype's reply is instant and a fabricated wait is invented state. The existing `prefers-reduced-motion` block (styles.css:3303) already collapses both animations (duration 0.01ms, iteration-count 1), so nothing extra is needed there.

**Verifier:** Evidence holds: `.sending-status` (styles.css:1246-1259) is the single rule for that selector, `font-size: 9px`, absolute above the composer, no animation, and no media query overrides it, so 9px applies at every viewport. The prototype window is real: App.jsx sendMessage awaits `gateway.sendMessage` (gateway.js:144 -> promise-queued synchronous localStorage mutate) then `refresh()` (also local), so `sending` is true for a few ms and the label mounts/unmounts inside roughly one frame — consistent with the 11.4ms/8.9ms MutationObserver numbers. But the prototype half is overstated: the deterministic reply is painted in the same beat, so no in-flight signal is needed there and nothing is perceived as flicker either (nothing is painted). The real cost is in Supabase mode: gateway.supabase.js:169 awaits a 6-query `fetchAll`, then App.jsx:275 calls `refresh()` (a second `fetchAll`), so `sending` lasts two network round-trips while ChatView.jsx:172 has already cleared the input and the send button is disabled — the 9px label is the only thing saying the message went. That is a legibility failure for the outdoor-phone brief, but it is transient (sub-second to ~1.5s) and in a path that currently returns a canned "fale comigo por VOZ" reply, so it is polish (P3), not a P2 annoyance. The fix is off in three places: (1) `toast-in` does not exist — the only keyframes are `dialog-rise`, `loading-pulse`, `voice-blink`, `voice-live-pulse` (styles.css:2099/2331/2483/2488), so the entrance animation as written is a no-op; (2) `loading-pulse` was written for the `.loading-avatar::after` ring (scale 0.82->1.1, opacity 0.85->0 held at 0 from 70% to 100%) — applied to the SVG itself it makes the icon vanish 30% of every cycle, i.e. it reintroduces blink; (3) the `Promise.all` 350ms hold fabricates a wait in the prototype where the reply is already computed, which collides with the brand contract's "Nada de estado inventado" (.impeccable.md:120) and delays `delivered` for nothing. Delayed appearance (CSS `animation-delay` with `both` fill) removes flicker without inventing latency.


### F19 · P3 · ADJUSTED — Resolved state keeps action-orange on the rail, the approval node and the 'Aguardando' icon; the desktop handoff flips colour with no transition

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:602-611 (.timeline-rail orange), :663-668 (.timeline-approval-node background orange), :903 (.causal-icon--pending), :3131-3183 (.desktop-timeline-handoff / .is-resolved, transition none); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/views/ChatView.jsx:73 and :104 (no resolved modifier)

**Viewports:** all (measured 390 and 1440)

**Evidence:** With both cases closed: .timeline-approval-node background rgb(255,90,54), border rgb(255,90,54); rail rgb(255,90,54); causal 'Aguardando' icon bg rgb(255,241,236) / color rgb(184,50,22) under the text 'decisão registrada'; desktop handoff becomes 'Decisão registrada / Nenhuma exceção pendente' with bg rgb(234,247,242) but computed transition 'all 0s' — the colour swaps in one frame.

**Why:** Brand contract: orange = action/decision point, teal = confirmed/controlled. After the decision the line should read as closed; 'A linha laranja conecta, o teal confirma' — the confirm step is exactly where a 240ms tint change earns its keep.

**Fix:** ChatView.jsx: (a) line 73 — `<span className={\`causal-icon${["pending","aguardando"].includes(context?.status) ? " causal-icon--pending" : " causal-icon--done"}\`}>` so the icon tint follows the same predicate as the text on line 74; (b) line 103 — `<span className={\`timeline-approval-node${approval ? "" : " is-resolved"}\`} aria-hidden="true" />`, reusing the exact `approval` binding the handoff already uses on line 104 (no new state, no dependency on F02 for correctness). styles.css, next to :663-668: `.timeline-approval-node.is-resolved { border-color: var(--teal); background: var(--teal); }` — leave .timeline-rail and the ::before/::after connectors orange (the line is the continuity motif per brand p.13; only the decision node is the action marker). Next to :903: `.causal-icon--done { color: var(--teal); background: var(--teal-soft); }` (same pair already used by .approvals-list-icon at :1856-1857). Optional motion, inside the existing ≥1200 block after :3183 and at the base rule: `.timeline-approval-node, .causal-icon, .desktop-timeline-handoff { transition: background-color var(--dur-med) var(--ease-out), border-color var(--dur-med) var(--ease-out), color var(--dur-med) var(--ease-out); }` — safe because the @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) block at :3303-3316 already forces transition-duration to 0.01ms. Do not touch the desktop handoff copy or the strip strings.

**Verifier:** Evidence is real and the CSS produces it: every measured colour is exactly a token (--orange #ff5a36 = rgb(255,90,54), --orange-soft #fff1ec = rgb(255,241,236), --orange-dark #b83216 = rgb(184,50,22), --teal-soft #eaf7f2 = rgb(234,247,242)); ChatView.jsx:73 hardcodes `causal-icon causal-icon--pending` regardless of state while line 74 flips the text to "decisão registrada"; .timeline-approval-node (styles.css:645-668) has no resolved modifier; styles.css has only four `transition` declarations (208, 2467, 3288 hover-only buttons, 3312 reduced-motion) and none touches .desktop-timeline-handoff, so `all 0s` is correct. Three things are overstated. (1) The rail: brand guide p.13 and .impeccable.md define the orange line as "continuidade operacional", not an action signal, so the rail staying orange after the decision is the brand behaviour, not a bug; the finding's own CSS does not recolour the rail either. (2) Reachability: the resolved timeline exists only in the prototype fixture path. src/data/model.js:439-440 and 525-526 set callContext.status to "aprovado"/"recusado"; in the live gateway (src/data/gateway.supabase.js:135-137) callContext is `null` when no approval is pending and always "aguardando" when one is, and ChatView's `hasOperationalTimeline = Boolean(callContext || pendingApproval)` then hides the whole timeline. So the deployed owner never sees this state today. (3) The text already says "decisão registrada" and the handoff already turns teal-soft, so the residual defect is icon/node colour semantics, not comprehension: P3 polish, not P2. The proposed binding also has a hazard: `approval` (first pending, global) and `context.status` (narrated case) diverge in the prototype after the first approval (flow-observations mobile item 4: John approved, Maria's card pending). Binding `is-resolved` on the timeline to context.status would paint the approval node teal next to an orange "Exceção pronta" handoff and a pending card. The node must follow the same `!approval` condition the handoff already uses at ChatView.jsx:104; only the causal icon should follow context.status, because it labels that text.

Leave the focus-ring list at :221-227 untouched (the later ≥1200 rule has equal specificity and wins; below 1200 the inspector is display:none). Verified ratios: card ink-900 on paper-warm 12.87:1, teal kicker 6.32:1, text-secondary 5.68:1. Drop the 'arrow lands on the card' rationale from the writeup.

**Verifier:** Evidence checks out against the source: styles.css:3095-3105 sets .context-inspector to height 100dvh + background var(--ink-950); :3134-3138 sets the card to var(--ink-900), border 1px rgba(255,255,255,.13), box-shadow none; :3005 grid 236px / minmax(620,780) / minmax(320px,1fr) gives 424px at 1440; :3108 .inspector-content padding 32px 24px with no vertical distribution, so the card (138-442 in the measured JSON) is top-aligned and ~458px of navy is empty. Computed contrast ink-900 vs ink-950 = 1.23:1 (finding correct); card vs paper on mobile 13.73:1; dark share at 1440 = 45.8% (and 59% at 1920 because the inspector track is 1fr and the card has no max-width — stretches to ~856px, which the finding missed). Brand guide p.13 quote is verbatim. Three things are overstated: (1) 'the arrow misses the card' — the handoff is a horizontal 'à direita' cue at y~497 vs card bottom 442; the card is the only object in a 424px column, nobody loses it, and its y is fixture-dependent (moves after every message/approval), so pointer alignment is not a real goal; (2) the 'contract forbids dark' argument is contestable: the .impeccable theme rule is written for the outdoor-phone landing, the inspector is literally the emphasis panel ('Agora / Sua decisão é necessária'), and the brand guide's own p.23 'PAINEL' application thumbnail is a dark dashboard — so this is RJ's call, not a rule violation; (3) impact: desktop is the secondary context for this user, buttons stay readable, nothing blocks or slows the decision — this is composition/figure-ground finish, i.e. P3 by the brief's own scale (P2 = annoyance, P3 = polish). The fix also has a real defect: narrowing the third track to minmax(320px,380px) caps the tracks at 1436px, leaving ~484px of unfilled paper at 1920 (justify-content default start), and the 1200-1359 block at :3224 redefines grid-template-columns so the narrowing would not even apply there. The rest of Option A is implementable and brand-safe (teal kicker on paper-warm 6.32:1, text-secondary 5.68:1, card on paper-warm 12.87:1, no copy or hierarchy change).


### F21 · P3 · ADJUSTED — Desktop handoff is an inert div dressed as an orange CTA (false affordance, not focusable) that duplicates the inspector heading 40px to its right

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:3155-3204 (.desktop-timeline-handoff grid 1fr/auto, min-height 62, padding 12 14, border 1px solid rgba(255,90,54,.32), background var(--orange-soft); :3175-3181 span color var(--orange-dark); :3187-3193 svg 24px var(--orange-dark)); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/views/ChatView.jsx:116-121 (plain div, no onClick/href); /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/App.jsx:307-319 (inspector h2 'Sua decisão é necessária')

**Viewports:** desktop 1440 (≥1200)

**Evidence:** Geometry 1440x900: handoff box 520x62 at y 466-528 ('EXCEÇÃO PRONTA / Decida no painel à direita ->') while the inspector already says 'Sua decisão é necessária' (28.8px) above the card at x 1041. The box has the visual grammar of a button (soft-orange surface, orange border, arrow) but no href/onClick and is skipped by Tab; 'à direita' is a visual reference with no programmatic link to the aside. .impeccable principle 1: 'Laranja é ação' and large orange in at most three places; flow-observations.md: 'duplicates what the inspector already says'. Severity: colour and IA lenses P2, layout lens P3; P2 kept for the false affordance (Nielsen #4) plus brand-rule violation.

**Why:** Orange is meant to tell the owner where to act; a non-interactive orange box trains him that orange sometimes does nothing and competes with the real action one column to the right. A keyboard user tabs past the element that looks like the way to decide. Two announcements for one decision is noise in a product whose promise is calm.

**Fix:** Keep both strings and both states; make the pointer honest and lighter without inventing a third surface. ChatView.jsx:116-120: when `approval` is truthy render `<button type="button" className="desktop-timeline-handoff" onClick={() => document.querySelector('.context-inspector .approval-actions button')?.focus()}>` (same three children); when resolved keep the current `<div className="desktop-timeline-handoff is-resolved" role="status">` — 'Nenhuma exceção pendente' is a status, there is nothing to jump to, and .approval-empty is a div that would need tabIndex=-1 to receive focus (the original fix's focus target does not work as written). styles.css ≥1200 block: `.desktop-timeline-handoff { grid-template-columns: auto minmax(0,1fr) auto; align-items: center; gap: 0 10px; min-height: 44px; padding: 8px 14px; text-align: left; font: inherit; cursor: default }` `button.desktop-timeline-handoff { cursor: pointer }` `button.desktop-timeline-handoff:focus-visible { outline: 3px solid var(--teal); outline-offset: 2px }` `.desktop-timeline-handoff span, .desktop-timeline-handoff strong { display: inline; grid-column: auto }` `.desktop-timeline-handoff svg { grid-row: auto; grid-column: auto; width: 18px; height: 18px }`. Keep background var(--orange-soft), border rgba(255,90,54,.32) and span/svg var(--orange-dark) — that is the stylesheet's existing 'pendente' status colour (same as .onboarding-cta and .approvals-status[data-status="pendente"]); switching to var(--white)+var(--border-strong) would create a one-off surface. Leave .is-resolved teal untouched. Pairs with F20 so the arrow points at the centred card.

**Verifier:** Facts check out: styles.css:3155-3193 (≥1200) gives .desktop-timeline-handoff a 520px-wide, min-height 62 grid on var(--orange-soft) with a 1px rgba(255,90,54,.32) border, radius 14, 24px var(--orange-dark) arrow; ChatView.jsx:116-120 renders it as a plain div with no onClick/href/tabIndex; App.jsx:309 puts 'Sua decisão é necessária' in the inspector; the 1440x900 geometry JSON confirms the box at 328,466 520x62 and the card at x 1041. But the interpretation is overstated. (1) 'Button grammar': in this stylesheet the pale tint + faint orange border + radius 14 is the established non-interactive STATUS/callout grammar, not the action grammar — .onboarding-cta (role="status", styles.css:1737-1746), .memory-suggestion-banner (:1702-1711), .causal-icon--pending, .approvals-status[data-status="pendente"], .status-pill--danger all use var(--orange-soft)+var(--orange-dark); real actions are solid var(--orange) with white text (.button--primary :1085). The handoff has no cursor:pointer and no hover state, so it does not pretend to be clickable; only the arrow implies 'go there'. Nielsen #4 at P2 does not hold. (2) Brand rule: 'manchas grandes de laranja' in .impeccable.md refers to saturated #ff5a36 surfaces on the landing page (hero signature, demo CTA, checkout); a #fff1ec tint used consistently as the 'pendente' state colour is not that violation. (3) Keyboard: the div being skipped by Tab costs nothing — the three real decision buttons in the inspector are already in tab order; a pointer element with no target need not be focusable. (4) The '40px to its right' is wrong: handoff right edge is x 848, inspector begins x 1016, card at x 1041 — ~170-190px. (5) Previous QA (design-qa.md item 7) explicitly accepted this element's two states. What survives: it is a redundant, oversized announcement (62px tall, second heading-weight strong) of a decision the inspector already announces, and its arrow suggests navigation with no programmatic link. That is polish on a secondary (≥1200) viewport for a phone-first user: P3, not P2.


### F22 · P3 · ADJUSTED — Orange connector from the approval node paints 8px over the navy card (and over the card/handoff at 768/1440); the mock's L stops at the card edge

**Where:** /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css:680-688 (.timeline-approval-node::after width = calc(var(--timeline-rail-column)/2 + var(--timeline-column-gap) - 6px) = 30px, z-index 3) vs :711-715 (.timeline-content--approval margin-left -8px, z-index 1), :644-657 + :690-695 (node z-index 3 above content)

**Viewports:** mobile 390, tablet 768, desktop 1440

**Evidence:** 390: ::after spans x 46-76 at y 540.9; card left edge x 68 -> 8px of orange 2px line over navy at 24px below the card top; document.elementFromPoint(73, 540.9) returns .timeline-approval-node (pseudo on top, z 3 > 1). 768: connector 142-172 vs card x 164. 1440: 306-336 vs handoff x 328. Mock @390: elbow ends at x 60.8, card starts 61.7 (1px). Severity: layout lens P2 (measured live at three viewports) vs mock lens P3 (derived from CSS, not visually verified); P2 kept on the live measurement.

**Why:** The orange line is the brand's one recurring motif ('não é enfeite: mostra continuidade operacional'); a stub bleeding into the decision card reads as misregistration on the most important object of the screen.

**Fix:** Keep the proposed token-based fix; it is correct at every breakpoint. In /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css: (1) add `--timeline-approval-inset: -8px;` to `.operational-timeline` (line 587 block); (2) `.timeline-content--approval { margin-left: var(--timeline-approval-inset); }` replacing the literal -8px at line 714; (3) `.timeline-approval-node::after { width: calc(var(--timeline-rail-column) / 2 + var(--timeline-column-gap) - 6px + var(--timeline-approval-inset)); }` at line 685. Verified results: 390 -> 22px (46->68, card edge 68); 768 -> 142->164 (card 164); 1440 -> 306->328 (handoff 328); <=359 (rail 48 / gap 8 from the max-width:359px block) -> 18px, 40->58, card at 10+48+8-8 = 58. No change to the >=768 or >=1200 blocks is required because the formula follows the tokens. If F06's wider card is adopted, only the token changes (-14px -> 16px line). Alternative one-liner if a token is not wanted: lower `.timeline-approval-node` below the content (e.g. `z-index: 1`, DOM order then lets the card paint over the stub), but this hides rather than ends the line and is less faithful to the mock's flush elbow, so prefer the token fix.

**Verifier:** The defect is real and the evidence checks out: recomputing from /Users/d1f/.codex/worktrees/ligou-v0.2-browser-pilot/Ligou.AI/dashboard/src/styles.css reproduces every measured coordinate. 390px: .conversation padding 14 -> grid x 14; node 12px centred in the 52px rail = x 34-46; .timeline-approval-node::after width = 52/2 + 10 - 6 = 30px -> x 46-76; content column starts at 76 and .timeline-content--approval margin-left:-8px puts the card at x 68 (matches mobile-390x844-geometry.json card box x 68 w 300) -> 8px of orange over navy. Vertically the line sits at row-top + 18 (approval offset) + 6 (half node) = 24px, below the card's 15px radius, so it crosses the flat navy edge. Paint order: node z-index 3 vs .timeline-content z-index 1 in the same stacking context, so the stub is on top; the card's overflow:hidden cannot clip a sibling's pseudo-element. 768: back-computing 24px chat-view padding from the measured 564px card gives node 130-142, line 142-172, card x 164 (8px overlap). 1440: conversation x 266 (measured) -> node 294-306, line 306-336, .desktop-timeline-handoff at 328 (measured) -> 8px over the soft-orange handoff. The '- 6px' in the width formula is exactly the node half-width, i.e. the line was tuned to stop at the grid column edge and the later -8px card margin was never propagated: an accidental misregistration, not a design choice. The approved mock (853x1844) shows the L-elbow stopping flush at the card edge, and the 'why' quote is verbatim in tmp/brand-guide-summary.md line 106. What does not hold is the severity: by the brief's own scale (P2 annoyance, P3 polish) an 8x2px orange stub 24px down the card edge is not something the owner reading on a phone in sunlight registers as an annoyance or that hinders the decision; the live measurement proves existence, not impact, and the finding's own mock lens already rated it P3. Live pixel check was not possible (localhost:4180 down), but the CSS derivation matching all three measured coordinates is sufficient corroboration.
